# 17b — Unit minds implementation plan

Opened 2026-10-03 for [doc 17](17-unit-minds.md) (decided 2026-10-02, `e30b9491`), the ledger and cut map
of the unit-minds programme, kept as [16b](16b-combat-implementation-plan.md) is kept for doc 16: a design
note per cut before it starts, an as-built row when it lands, the exact commands run, a reviewer that did
not write the cut. Doc 17 §22 is the build order and the authority on each milestone's scope and gate;
this document records how each one was built. The cuts are named as doc 17 names them (M1a, M1b, M2 to
M8, L); sub-cuts are M1a.1, M1a.2 and so on.

---

## 0. Build discipline

16b §0 applies unchanged: the workspace green at every sub-cut (`cargo test --workspace --exclude
mush-bot` and `cargo test -p mush-bot` on its own), the contract gates re-blessed in the commit that
moves them, all-target Clippy, strict rustdoc, one commit and one push per sub-cut, no second lane, no
version ladders, no fixture impersonating a production module, panic policy unchanged, a non-baseline
stanza for every new verb. The programme adds:

1. **No Rhai before M2.** M1a and M1b are native. A production match stays on `Controller::Reflex`
   until M4's flip; `Controller::Mind` units exist before M4 only in fixtures and the twin lanes.
2. **The twin gates** (doc 17 §17): T0 is the shipped reflex, T1 the native stock mind, T2 the Rhai
   stock mind. Gate A is T1 ≡ T2 exactly (hashes, intents, the cross-tempo fixture). Gate B bounds
   T0 against T2 over pooled seeded matches with a Wilson interval. Every divergence between T0 and
   T1 is named in doc 16 §11 and doc 17 §24 in the cut that introduces it.
3. **The parity rule for the view** (D-UM-4): a mind reads exactly what the cockpit keyframe carries;
   every mind field is first a `Frame` and keyframe field with a `FRAME_VERSION` bump, the row
   formatters produce the same sentences before and after any move (goldens and the bot's
   `arena_contract.rs` pin them), and a tier truncates, never extends.
4. **The hash.** A cut that moves `state_hash` re-blesses the cross-tempo fixtures once, in its last
   sub-cut, with the reason in its ledger row. Before M4 the hash of a production match must not move;
   a variant added to a hashed enum that changes an encoding is a declared re-bless, not a silent one.
5. **Performance.** Both harness lanes hold their lenses at every sub-cut; M4's `tacsim_harness_arena_minds`
   is the P3 gate (doc 17 §18) with its one fast-path trigger; a cut that adds per-pulse work reports
   the harness lines verbatim in its gate.
6. **The bot stays external.** `crates/mush-bot/AGENTS.md` holds: the engine's native stock mind, the
   view and the kernels share no code with the bot; the bot's reader and contract test move with
   declared reader changes, and `cargo test -p mush-bot` is in the gate of any sub-cut that changes a
   line-mode sentence or the keyframe.
7. **Where the record goes.** The 17b ledger row (§3) takes the as-built cells; doc 17 §24 takes the
   as-built amendments to the minds spec; doc 16 §11 takes the ones that touch the arena spec (the
   verb table, the frame, the controller, the deliberation layer); doc 17 §22's milestone paragraph
   gains one line, "Landed <date> at <hash>; see 17b section 3, row X."; 16c rows the cut closes are
   marked as 16c's convention has it.

---

### Through M6 continuation plan of 2026-10-17

[17d](17d-unit-minds-through-m6-plan.md) plans the two explicit post-M5 cuts
(M5rules and M5r) and M6 in ten sub-cuts, planned from `1137c373` and rebased onto
IC10 at `7435f954` before implementation. M5 and L remain
landed; M5rules is accepted at `e93a4a1c`, with its Windows and Linux gates
recorded below. M5r is accepted on 2026-10-17 with its evidence in §5. M6 preparation remains
separate from both accepted cuts. The plan's implementation choices
include early draft/revision ownership in a minimal `mush-roll`, general authored
cards and rules, input recording, proving and bench tools, and the client surfaces.

The owner's 2026-10-17 direction makes the recorded timing misses nonblocking:
keep measurements and investigate repeatable regressions, without holding this
programme on isolated host variability or three consecutive timing passes.
Determinism, op/resource bounds, observer isolation and separation of proving from
live work remain hard gates. This supersedes the outstanding timing-sign-off
wording in the historical M4/M5 rows, without changing their recorded results.

M7 waits for the separate [economy-stability checkpoint](17d-unit-minds-through-m6-plan.md#7-trade-run-coordination-and-the-m7-checkpoint)
after M6. The owner's later direction on 2026-10-17 authorizes continuing into M7
once that checkpoint passes and its design note records the economic interfaces.
The cut-map rows below keep
the original milestone scope; 17d supplies the current execution sequence and
explicit compatibility departures from 17c's post-M5 assumptions.

---

## 3. The cut ledger

One row per cut, a single very long line, appended with the cut's design note before its first sub-cut
and filled by its record when it lands. A cell holds no pipe and no newline; "Landed" names each
sub-cut with its hash and what it built; "Evidence" the loop each sub-cut went through, the gate
results, the sweep numbers, the harness lines, and what was not run; "Deviations" every departure
from doc 17 or the plan and the residuals still open.

| Cut | Design note | Landed | Evidence | Deviations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1a | 2026-10-03 (doc 17 §22's M1a, the first cut of the unit-minds programme, on the owner's word of the same day "set up 17b and run M1a"; planned read-only from `32a1cc23`, the 17b opening commit, and revised at the same HEAD after a feasibility and an adversarial review whose dispositions are the plan's section 8; the programme lead's note of 2026-10-03 outranks doc 17's prose where they differ and this plan follows it: NO Rhai and no `mush-mind` crate, a production match stays on `Controller::Reflex` and `Controller::Mind` units exist only in fixtures and the twin lane, the production `state_hash` does not move, the bot stays external with `cargo test -p mush-bot` in the gate of every sub-cut that moves a sentence or the keyframe, `reflex.rs` loses nothing of its decision half and no test is deleted, the lance-leader and the fire law are not this cut). **The seam, no Rhai: the motor extracted, the row formatter moved, the frame grown for a reader that is not a person, `DRIVE` for pilots, the mind seam with a native T1 stock mind beside the reflex, and the twin measured.** THE ONE OWNER QUESTION, Q1, put before M1a.4 starts: the verb cannot be `GOTO` — `mush-core` registers the baseline command `goto` with aliases `go` and `move` and `Registry::insert_entry` refuses a duplicate name or alias with `Collision`, so a `goto` selector fails the tacsim module at boot; the default is **`DRIVE`** (word `drive`, no collision at HEAD; doc 17 §4 already calls the motor the drive-by-wire), a declared departure (f) from D-UM-10 whose Rhai-side `goto(..)` function name is M2's and unchanged. Re-verified at HEAD: `reflex.rs` is 7,042 lines with its driving half interleaved with its decision half, the avoid TTL sweep at the top of `decide` and `follow` mutating the search budget and the A* scratch; `state_hash` folds the controller byte (`0` pilot, `1` reflex), the reflex rows per row with no count prefix, and the due heap's COUNT alone; `DueEvent` orders by `(t_ms, seq, kind)` with `seq` unique, so a kind's place in the enum orders nothing; `apply_control` raises the epoch first for every state and a `Live` always flips the unit to `Pilot`; `Arena::order` drops every resolver's `Err(Refusal)` with `let _`; `Frame` has no order and no arena time; `FRAME_VERSION` is 1 with the web and the TUI reading named keys and no version; the TUI contacts pane is 46 columns inside at the 120-column floor; `brief_lines` prints `contact_sentence`, not `ContactRow::render`; `Classification::Classified { team }` resolves the side, not the chassis; `UnitState.fatal_sections` is `fatal_section_mask` over the chassis definition; the kernels `stand_off_cell`, `entry_cell`, `far_entry_cell`, the open-line walks, `ordered_target`, `best_hostile_where`, `ranges`, `stand_off_want` are private; `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` is 2 and `af1_the_snapshot_shape_is_pinned` moves only with it; the pre-pulse buffer hands continuous orders over in key order; `COCKPIT_VERBS` is fifteen. THE HASH, by construction and pinned: `Controller::Mind` is code 2 with 0/1 unchanged; `DueKind::MotorTick`, `MindThink` and `MindLand` are never scheduled in production and the heap folds its count; the motor fields leave `ReflexUnit` for one `Arena.motors: MotorTable` but the reflex-row fold reads them back from the table in the same order so a reflex row folds the same bytes, and the two new fold sections (motor rows of non-reflex units, mind rows) are per-row with no count prefix so an empty set adds no byte; M1a.1 pins the first and last hash of `tests/reflex.rs`'s 1,000 ms replay and the final hash of a shipped two-a-side duel at HEAD before the move and M1a.4, M1a.5 and M1a.6 re-assert both constants; no re-bless anywhere. doc 17 section 24); `set_controller`'s release of the motor row and `MotorTick` on every transition moves the production hash on one reachable path, a pilot's `DRIVE` followed by a `Released` control (`EXIT` or a linkdead grace running out), so the plan head's "the production hash does not move" holds for the pinned lanes and that path is pinned by `m1a5_a_pilots_drive_dies_with_the_released_seat` alone; a mind's successful `LOCK` stages no `Locked` notice (it reads its lock off `view.lock`); a mind's order expires only at a landing, so a think between the deadline and the next landing still reads it as standing and a runner that overflows every think never expires one; `mind_land` returns early for an ended match after taking the scratch without clearing the row's `pending`; `Arena::mind_view` is `pub` and mutates hashed state (drains the buffer, settles ranges), so a timed run must use a sampled clone; `fatal_section_mask` is computed in `frame_scope` for every held row on every frame, unarmed hulls and readingless rows included (wasted work scaling with contacts by pilots; the fix is one move into the `is_some_and` closure); the web decoder invents `front` for a frame with no `aspect` key and prints a side word plus `-` where the TUI prints no tail, so the two clients disagree on a version-1 frame retained across an upgrade; `drive_arrived` and `drive_no_route` carry no goal cell, so T1 attributes them to its memory goal, exact only while `decide_ms == STOCK_THINK_MS` and the motor runs before the mind at a millisecond, and a `Refused` notice for a `Goal` is never read; a turret-killed T1 hull reads unarmed (no weapon rows) rather than dry and so patrols and hunts where T0 withdraws, a fourteenth divergence counted by none of the thirteen counters; a hull whose `engage_cm` is under one 50 m range step can never enter Engage under T1 (shipped content clear; the twin fixture's sight raised from 5 to 10 cells for it); `order_hint` and `support` are computed only with an issued `Order`, which the pool never passes, so those zeros and `order_end`/`deg_round` over `ordered_thinks=0` are upper bounds; the `Order`/`ReleaseOrder` arms branch `if controller == Mind` rather than exhaustively, so a fourth controller would take the reflex branch silently; `ReflexMemory::set_order` can still create a reflex row with no motor row and `MotorTable` derives `Default` (window 0) beside `new()` (window `i64::MIN`), both harmless states production never reaches; `web/src/lib/replay.ts` labels any unit without the `m` flag `pilot`, so a Mind hull (flag `n`) would read `pilot` in a replay (a client follow-up). *Test gaps:* `fall_back_to_waypoint` is `pub` as an audited kernel with no alone/crowded audit of its own; At this record, `cargo test -p mush-contract-tests --test hex_vocabulary`: `test result: ok. 2 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 27.52s`. **Not run**: a hand-played match on this build (the owner plays between cuts; nothing of L has been fought by a person, and the live dev box has not been redeployed — the deploy is the server half alone and must fall between matches, since a format-5 snapshot is refused by name at resume and the match voided); no real model call anywhere (`two_process_llm` drives a scripted provider across the IPC seam; no persona's brief has reached a model); no production path sets `Controller::Mind`, so the widened `squads_of` and the self-charge re-key ran in the L.2 fixtures and the twin lane alone, and no pilot has read a persona's lapse from a live match; no ordered twin fixture (the twin's `support` and order counters stay at 0 over `ordered_thinks` 0, so the new shadow arm has never run); no test of the `engage` expiry word on a self-charged Mind hull, of `leaders_row`'s `?` and no-record branches, or of `MatchRecord.leaders`' defaulted decode of a pre-L.1 document; Linux not measured. | **L.1**: (1) `admit_order`'s lead arm requires the named roster to `holds(unit)` where the brief asked only for a roster whose id matches — stricter, never weaker, the cockpit's own `ORDER` fans out to exactly that roster's units, and without it a direct caller could order red-1 onto a unit outside red-1; pinned by the lead-onto-own-tank and lead-onto-blue-machine cases. (2) The Persona arm likewise requires the record's roster of `order.squad` to hold `unit` (the correctness review's F2, fixed in the same pass): the reply path fans out to exactly that roster, so production behaviour is unchanged and only a direct caller aiming a persona's order outside its squad now reads `NoCharacter`; the committer records both clauses as built in doc 17 §24's D-UM-52 line. (3) Tests 6, 7 and 12 live in `tests/cockpit.rs` rather than `tests/deliberation.rs` because the multi-pilot Y.3 `Rig` is there, and the no-squads half of test 2 is `l1_a_pilots_only_match_mints_no_leader` in the same file since the deliberation file's lobby roster always fills with AI. (5) Literal counts differ from the brief's inventory — `record.rs` has 2 `MatchRecord` test literals not 3, `tests/cockpit.rs` 0 not 2 (it reads records through helpers), `request.rs` 0 `SquadBrief` literals not 3 (built through `from_signal`), `world_task.rs` one `probe_squad_brief` helper serving two tests not 2 literals; the compiler found every one. (9) The report pasted about sixteen of the eighty-two harness lines; the gate's log holds them all and the gate re-ran the harness. **The sweep repair** (`3ef6e34a`): the sweep's two guesses at the TUI cockpit failure (a part-painted pane; load) were close but wrong about the mechanism — `Term::mark()` is `seen.len()` and `seen` grows only when the test reads, so bytes the server already sent land after the next mark; the race was in the tests and production was fine; `supervision_abandoned` and the harness lens trip were judged not repairs. **Residuals still open** (the two judges' lists merged and de-duplicated with the plan's section 6; what this record closed is marked). (R1, L.1) The `Character` arm of `admit_order` accepts any issuer (it does not require `order.issuer == Some(seat.unit)`) and does not apply the cockpit's `require_alive`, so a direct caller could write a lead's hint in another unit's contact scope or admit a wrecked lead's character; beyond the brief, and the cockpit still guards both for typed orders. (R2, L.1) `forward_order` counts a `NoCharacter` from `admit_order` as not offered and the cockpit turns an empty reach into `CockpitError::Order(Busy)`; its doc does not mention the per-unit admission, so if the admission and `command_of` ever disagree the pilot reads `Busy`, not the `NoCharacter` sentence (pinned unreachable from a cockpit today). (R3, L.1, declared) A reply requested before the leader's hull was wrecked still passes the record-only `Persona` admission and lands on the survivors (`command.rs` skips a wrecked commander at the signal, not the reply). (R4, L.1, declared) The watches, `by` included, are registry RAM and an AF.3 resume drops them while a standing order's `by` survives in the snapshot; and a lead's `RELEASE` drops a persona's order from every unit but not the persona's watch (`note_order_released` keys on the releaser), so its lapse may still be said later (pre-existing). (R5, L.1) `MatchRecord.leaders`' `#[serde(default)]` decode of a pre-L.1 document and its survival through the live document are unpinned by any test (the format-6 bump voids pre-L.1 snapshots, so the live risk is low). (R6, L.1) The in-source arena-library tests (`intents.rs`, `step.rs`, `motor.rs`, `reflex.rs`, `arena_task.rs`) and `tests/arena.rs` write author literals inline with a `9_001` stand-in rather than through a helper; a convention change touches every site. (R7, L.1) `l1_a_wrecked_leaders_hull_drops_its_squad_to_doctrine` sets the wreck flag before pulse 0 rather than wrecking mid-match, so it characterises `command.rs`'s existing skip rather than a wreck after a first ask. (R8, L.2) `crates/tacsim/src/arena/reflex.rs:941` still says "the link dropped" in the 16b cut N.2 comment on the `order_expired` journalling block (the brief's grep for `mind link` could not catch it and the file was not on the brief's list); a one-line reword before M3 lands `MindDropped`; code, so not closed by this record. (R9, L.2) The per-squad watch is latest-wins (`note_human_order`'s `insert`, pre-existing, 16c Q43): a pilot whose watch a teammate's later `ORDER` overwrote is never told their own order lapsed and since L.2 no longer hears the later issuer's lapse either, so `help cockpit`'s "you alone are told" holds only for the order the squad's watch last recorded; declared in doc 16 section 11 by this record beside the per-unit-watch candidate. (R10, L.2) A Mind squad's `SquadSignal` states histogram is empty (`take_deliberation_signals` counts states over reflex rows only) while every member row reads `Startup`, so a persona's brief for a Mind squad has no `doing:` line beside members all at startup; reachable only in the L.2 fixtures until M4, which revisits the signal for minds (the engine reads no mind memory, D-UM-49). (R11, L.2) The declared `engage` expiry word for a self-charged Mind hull has no test (`charge_run`'s TTL is 600,000 ms and the run stops at the first fired line), and a reflex hull's frame-time `frame_order` under a self-charge now also reads `Engage` (no reader today); a short-TTL pin is a cheap 16c item. (R12, L.2) The twin shadow's new self-charge arm never runs (`shadow` is called with `issued` None; `ordered_thinks=0`), so its correctness rests on reading, and the old arm's `of == unit` or-clause is dead code kept as the brief asked; vacuous until an ordered twin fixture exists (M4's gate). (R13, L.2) The cross-tempo fixture's `Startup` pin is probably met by the t=0 `NoOrder` signal alone (`deliberate_min_ms` defaults to 30,000 and the run stops at 30,000 ms); nothing asserts a red-1 signal after the order or an `order_expired` line inside the run, though its doc says the order also runs out inside the run; and M1a's residual (2), a re-key to an unclassified held row, stays the lead's ruling, pinned neither way. (R14, L.2) `leaders_row`'s `?` (gone character) and no-booked-record branches are untested; the bot contract case pins the two lapse sentences as literals rather than through the private `lapse_line`, so a later wording change could leave the bot case stale. (R15, L.2) The pre-existing `y1_running_match` helper seats pilots through `slot_seated` before any `mush_sim` seat exists, leaving `record.teams[*].pilots` empty and the pilots' tanks dealt into squads as `Reflex`, so the L.1 and Y.1 tests built on it pass on a shape production never makes; L.2's `l2_running_match` boards for real, and moving the older tests onto it is a follow-up for the lead. (R16, L.2) `tests/twin.rs`'s support bullet rewording ("the charge held or — the charge being the hull itself — re-keyed to Engage") reads awkwardly; comment prose only. (R17, the plan: 16c candidates listed here and not filed) The `ordered` tape line does not carry `by`, so the debrief cannot name the orderer; `MatchDeliberation::human_holds` is a name for a rule no longer about humans (rename with `NotReflex → NotMachine` at M4); `SquadBrief.commander` is still the budget actor of a persona's call; the `order` stanza's note in `legacy-handlers.toml` still says the LLM seam; the watch is per squad, so a wingman's lapse is prefixed `Squad red-1:`; no NPC `ObjKind::Npc` lance-leader object exists (IC; the corridor's dispatch design), the leader's name reaches no pilot surface but the lapse, `Effect::ModuleAsync`'s actor is unchanged, and a mind's state word comes off no memory. (R18, the sweep) The CI window lens has thin headroom on this box (9.8 to 11.9 ms of 12 on recent sweeps; one trip at 12.519 in round 1 with every deterministic counter identical); for the owner or a reviewer, not a repair. **Closed by this record**: L.1's residual that the mushd `observer_rule` TUI cockpit test is a standing paint-timing flake (the sweep repair `3ef6e34a` fixed the race in the test: the `STATUS` poll read whole and the wait on the pane's last row); the committer's owed text — doc 16 W7's row, §6's Deliberation and Provider-failure paragraphs, the Y.1, M1a.5 and M1a.6 entries' three false clauses amended in place and the §11 entries for L.1 and L.2; doc 17 §24's D-UM-52/53 as built (the stricter `holds(unit)` clause on both arms included), D-UM-32's retired sentence, D-UM-15's self-charge amendment and D-UM-53's "stays `Controller::Mind`" read against the lead's note, and §22's L line. 16c: nothing closed (the design note's "Nothing closed" holds; the candidates in R17 are listed, not filed). |

the card pair refusal pinned at one cadence; a dropped think recording no `ThinkStat`; `cargo test -p mush-bot` in the gate of M2.2 and M2.7 only (the sweep ran it; no sub-cut moved a sentence or the keyframe). | **M2.1**: (1) `budget::check(ops)` counts one operation per call and ignores the number Rhai reports instead of storing it as the brief wrote, and the post-return re-check is the new `budget::tripped()` rather than `check(spent().0)`: review finding F1 — a package method's callback runs on a cloned `GlobalRuntimeState` (`types/fn_ptr.rs:444`) whose count restarts from the caller's and is discarded, so trusting the argument let sibling callbacks spend unbounded operations; `self.progress` has one call site (`data_check.rs:197`), so the count of calls is the count of operations, deterministic as before, and once tripped every later operation trips again. (2) `system_error` also latches the think's first refusal in a thread-local that `Sandbox::think` clears at its start and reads ahead of every end but a panic, on the `Ok` path too: review finding F2 — `wrap_err` wraps even a system error from a callback in a catchable `ErrorInFunctionCall`. (3) The fault map classifies `error.unwrap_inner()` rather than the outer variant, and a wrapped `Exit` is a return: F2 again, so a termination, a limit or an exit inside a callback keeps its meaning. (4) `Fault::Type` texts and trace lines are one bounded line (120 characters, control characters spaced, rendering stopped at the width): review finding F3 — a thrown 8,192-element array was formatted whole outside the budget. (5) `ErrorTooManyFunctions` does not exist in rhai 1.26.0 (the function cap is parse-time, `parser.rs:3337`), so the `Limit` arm covers five variants and a 33rd function is `CompileError::Parse` (test 18). (6) `MindRefusal`'s Display is `refused`, so every refusal renders `<text>: refused` (rhai writes `ErrorSystem` as `{name}: {err}`); the brief's literal constructor kept, the suffix declared for M2.3's words. (7) `pub use rhai;` is re-exported so M2.3 registers its API with the pinned types without its own rhai edge (not in the brief; optional). (8) `clamp(v, lo, hi)` with `lo > hi` is refused through `system_error` (the brief is silent; `i64::clamp` would panic). (9) `decode` is stricter than the brief's list: unsorted or duplicate keys, leading zeros, `-0`, i64 overflow, trailing bytes and empty input are refused, so only bytes `encode` could write are accepted. (10) FLOAT detection in `from_map` is recursive through arrays and nested maps, reading "a FLOAT anywhere" literally. (11) Tests beyond the brief's sixteen: 17 (callbacks and cause words), 18 (the backstop before the budget and the function cap), 19 (bounded fault text), and extensions to 2 (`Outcome.ops`/`fee`), 3 (MoreString exclusion, the exact FLOAT text `Type("x")`), 9 (the strip proof on an engine where `print` resolves with an uncleared control, after the first proof was found vacuous), 14 and 15 (through a map callback); the in-source package test gains `"k" + 1`, `"abc".len()` and a function pointer's `name`. (12) Doc 17 is left for the committer: §7's Lints paragraph lists five denies where the crate denies six; §8's memory `mem` in scope is built as `this` bound through `bind_this_ptr` to `fn think(view, card)`; §7's abs/min/max are the packages' own, not native helpers; §24 should record no string + INT in scripts (`"k" + i.to_string()`), the function cap as parse-time, `exit` as a return, the budget's own count over callbacks, the per-think refusal latch and the bounded `type` texts. (13) Deferred: the AST cache never evicts (M3's seal store); `EngineLimits::default()` is Class I's 1,500 with no `ops_limit <= max_ops_backstop` check, so M2.4 sets the backstop to the largest class ceiling; `Compiled`'s fields stay public as the brief wrote them; `MemError::Key` carries the full key, bounded only by the 8,192-byte string backstop; callbacks through a bare script-function name remain reachable (charged and latched), removal a lead question. (14) The builder's report filed the FLOAT text under decisions and gave no line numbers for the restriction_lints changes (`:107`, `:131-133`, `:446-454`, `:479-488`); the fixer restored the brief's `Type(key)`. **M2.2**: (1) `tests/perception.rs`'s seven whole `arena.definition = walled.definition.clone()` assignments are unchanged rather than `Arc::new(walled.definition.clone())`: `walled` is an `Arena`, so the right-hand side is already an `Arc` clone and the brief's spelling would not compile; the decode and terrain sites the brief did not list (`tests/arena.rs` 8, `deliberation.rs`, `shipped_scale.rs`, `sight.rs`, `minds.rs`, `snapshot.rs`'s in-source test, `arena_bridge.rs`) were changed as the type demanded. (2) `NativeStockMind::think` passes `job.view` and `job.map` by auto-deref, not `&**job.view`/`&**job.map`: clippy's `explicit_auto_deref` refuses the brief's spelling; identical behaviour. (3) `StockCard::pair(&self, named: &'static str)` is a new public method beyond the brief and both preset arms (`apply_mind_card`, `@mind/set`) now pair-check a preset by its canonical name: the review found `Rearguard` (`break_off_pct` 70) breaks the pair on a config-admitted doctrine with `fight_home_pct` above 70, which ruling (b)'s "the presets pair-valid" cannot allow; refused by name, never clamped; the default and shipped doctrines are unaffected and the twin is identical. (4) `kill_duel` fields a second blue hull out of red's sight: with blue alone the kill ended the skirmish inside its substep and no later `WeaponReady` or pass was ever evaluated, so the brief's cross-tempo proof was vacuous; the builder's deviation that "some later step of the shot path refused the wreck" is withdrawn (nothing in the shot path refuses a wreck; with the rule reverted the tape shows `fired` at 6,000 and 7,000), and the `Contact` arm stays with the in-source warrant test because a `Contact` slot is `Unlawful` at the arena in M1b. (5) The ruling (c) conditions fixture moves blue by one hand (`poked_replay` at 10,000, on every cadence's lattice) and runs the three `Every` drives only, not the task drive: under continuous motion the slot fires at an intermediate rounded range (590), never at "once the rounded range reads 550"; its bound is compiled through `cockpit::parse_arming` so the arena test fails if the strict compile reverts. (6) The strikes baseline's hand reset asserts the figure is already on the row before writing it: in the new code the fault and the overflow share one branch, so the hand is a no-op and the equality is structural; there is no pre-edit record of M1a's overflow hashes. (7) The arena-side `mind_card_refused` pair check lives in `tests/minds.rs` beside `card_run`, not in `tests/cockpit.rs`, and runs at one cadence; the `@mind/set` strings are in `tests/cockpit.rs`'s existing M1b.5 test, the rig's card being Sniper (Line's pair). (8) `m1a6_memory_check_refuses_by_key_count_and_value` drops the float, `u64::MAX`, object, nested-array and mixed-array refusals because `MemValue` cannot express them, adding the ASCII refusals; the arena-level decode of such bytes to the empty map is pinned only for `not json`, `[1, 2]` and the whitespace case. (9) `m1b5_every_slider_refuses_out_of_range_and_off_step_by_name` takes `break_off_pct`'s low end after `fight_home_pct 0` and the halves acceptances under `break_off_pct 80`, with `fight_home_pct 100` now a `Pair` refusal: ruling (b)'s consequence; `fight_home_pct`'s range sentence still says 0 to 100 though values above 80 are always refused by the pair, a wording question for the lead. (11) `range > 200 AND range < 201` now compiles to the empty window (201, 200) and is accepted; not refused, deferred. (12) The `fire` stanza's note in `crates/mush-contract-tests/legacy-handlers.toml` and `crates/mush-mind/src/memory.rs`'s module doc were edited beyond the brief's file list to stop them describing the cockpit count pre-check and the "until M2.2" codec; the baseline count did not move. (16) A possess-then-release lays down a fresh row and un-drops a dropped hull, and a decode reinstalls the native runner: declared M3's. **M2.3**: (1) `goto` is registered as custom syntax `goto ( $expr$ )`, not a function: rhai 1.26.0's tokenizer table marks `goto` reserved and not callable, so `goto(..)` cannot resolve as a call; the script spelling is unchanged, the argument is evaluated as any expression and handed to the one Rust `goto`, and the name is recorded only when rhai accepts the registration so the pin would show a refusal. (2) `arena/stock_mind.rs` was touched, by visibility alone, against the plan's adv F12 resolution: `line_between`, `own_point`, `stand_off_of` and `contact` went private → `pub(crate)` with a doc line each because the brief's kernel rule requires wrapping exactly these functions; no body changed, the twin at release is byte-identical, and the harness was not run. (3) `Weapon.range_band` answers the four INTs `[index, of, max_range_cm, dispersion_mrad]` or `()`, not a word: `FrameBand` and the keyframe's `range_band` object carry no word. (4) `Receiver.reach_permille` is floored to the keyframe's `reach_percent` times ten (D-UM-4: a tier never extends; the fold's raw per mille would hand a mind a digit no pilot sees) under the brief's getter name so the pin and `API_VERSION` hold; `MindView` itself is unchanged; a D-UM-4 reading for doc 17 §24. (5) `arm` bounds two terms tighter than their field's type, `min_permille <= 1000` and `aspects` in `1..=ASPECTS_ANY`, refused `type` (D-UM-10: a mind arms no slot a pilot's `FIRE … WHEN` cannot write; a mask with bits past the four facets would not round-trip the keyframe's words); `min_range_m > max_range_m` is not refused because `parse_arming` accepts an empty range too. (6) `context::with` answers `Result<R, Box<EvalAltResult>>`, not the brief's bare `R`, since refusing `no think is running` needs an error to return; it also refuses when the context is already borrowed. (7) `ThinkContext::of(job, decide_ms)` was added beyond the brief so M2.4 builds a context in one call; untested here. (8) `contact(v, id)` with an unheld id answers `()`, not `unknown`: it is the row lookup and not held is its answer, so it never builds a latching refusal. (9) A kernel's REQUIRED arguments are typed INT/bool/string/Array, so a FLOAT there is rhai's own catchable not-found rather than a latched `type` refusal; only the stations and the `()`-able kernel arguments are `Dynamic` (declared in `kernels.rs`' head). (10) The array, map, string and iterator packages' methods are pinned by package, not name by name, because rhai lists natives only under its `metadata` feature; `PACKAGE_NAMES` carries fifteen names. (11) `Notice` gains getters for the `slot_held` detail keys `why`, `floor`, `range_m`, `min_m`, `max_m`, `rounds`, `keeps`, within the brief's one getter per field; the text-only parts of `Struck`, a `Contact` notice's class and a `SectionLost` notice's consequences are not exposed. (12) Three PARITY rows name a divergence the live test must carry: `Order.target`/`of` answer the stored id where the keyframe prints null once the row is unheld, `View.notices` is unfiltered where the keyframe drops empty-text notices, `Notice.friendly` reads the view's twelve rows. (13) `best_hostile`'s admit is a verbatim copy of T1's stand closure rather than a lifted shared function, pinned by nine cases. (14) The getter-value reads and the 194-name resolution run on a scratch engine with the seven packages and `register_api`, while the stations, kernels, fees, context functions and card run through `standard_sandbox().think`. (15) `arm` with an INT target is not checked against the view's rows; the landing's `Unlawful` is the one wall. (16) Nothing under `docs/design` was edited; the committer writes the `goto`, `range_band`, `reach_permille` and `arm`-bound facts into doc 17 §24 and this row. **M2.4**: (1) The (j)-field rig was COPIED into `tests/minds_rhai.rs`, not lifted into `tests/support/mod.rs`, because `support` already exports a `tank(world, team, slot)` of another signature and lifting would re-plumb the 7,000-line `tests/minds.rs` in a cut not asked to touch it; `support` exports nothing new and M2.5's `TwinRunner` will have to lift or copy it again. (3) The float probe's exact bits are not a bare `==` against the pinned literals: rhai 1.26.0 without `unchecked` compares FLOATs within `f64::EPSILON` relative, so a one-ULP powf difference would read equal; the exact bit tests `(v - exact) * 1000000.0 == 0.0` (absolute EPSILON against zero), and six closing bits pin the tolerance itself; the probe also emits `startup()` on its first think so its helm turns the hull. (4) The resumed fixture pins the tempo `Quiet` every pulse through `stepped`: the (j) field is pinned at construction, the snapshot carries the clock's tempo and signals (16b AF.1) but deliberately not the test pin, so without the re-pin the decoded clock journals `Quiet` to `Engaged` one substep after the resume; this is the dropped test lever, not an engine gap, and an unpinned match resumes hash-continuous, so nothing goes to doc 16 §11 or 16c for it. for 17b §2: the `twin_rhai_*` lines above. **Residuals still open after M2.7** (the seven judges' lists merged, de-duplicated, with what later sub-cuts closed left out: M2.4's live parity test closed M2.3's scratch-engine gap and pinned `trace` in no ledger line; M2.6 filled the fire half, so `Flier::Twin`'s panic on an armed hull and the lanes' `fire_only` allowance are gone). **For the lead** — (1) the ops ceiling: lane 3's ops-plus-fee max is 1,274 with 273 of 6,036 thinks past 1,200 (the costliest armed Engage thinks on 9- to 12-row views, `arming_for` about 190 of it) against class I's 1,500 with no bound asserted in that lane, while lanes 1, 2, the extra lane and `tests/minds.rs` assert 1,200 as a STOP and the 2v2 margin under it is 21 ops after four cost-only helm rewrites: whether lane 3 must hold 1,200 (script cost cuts) or class I's 1,500; and Class 0's 400 (D-UM-35) is exceeded on about 91 per cent of thinks (ops alone p50 708, p99 1,123; ops plus fee p50 772 to 992), about three times the ROM budget §18 gives Standard Doctrine, the forward STOP for M3/M4 where `minds_stock_ci` runs it at Class 0 (plan section 4, Q2), beside the script's own measured rate of 11.9 ops/µs alone (9.7 in the parallel gate run), about half of §18's 19.5 to 24.1 on the blessing host itself with `Timed` warming the cache on 1,320 of 16,602 thinks — report-only, but it bears on M4's pool sizing; `arming_words` cannot move without the goldens, the bot and the web), and `fight_home_pct`'s range sentence still promising 0 to 100 in halves while `break_off_pct` tops at 80 so any value above 80 is refused `Pair` on every card; (4) `range > 200 AND range < 201` compiling to the empty window (201, 200), a slot that can never fire, accepted and replied `between 200 and 201 m` (it fired at 200 and 201 before M2.2) — refuse `min > max` as `NotThisCore` in a later cut (the inverted-window refusal M1b.4 left open, with the `held_said` prune, still unfiled in 16c); (6) a bare script-function name is still a function pointer for the array and map packages' callback methods (`map`, `filter`, `sort`, `reduce`, `retain` and the rest) — every operation they run is charged and a refusal inside one is latched, but removing the surface would shadow package methods against the seven-package rule; (7) `RhaiRunner::on`'s doc offers M4's per-worker sandbox as a use, which D-UM-22 does not settle. **For M3** — the AST cache never evicts (one entry per `(digest, abi)` for the life of the process; player minds re-sealed at every edit each leave an AST resident); `EngineLimits::default()` and `standard_sandbox()`'s backstop are class I's 1,500 while one engine serves every class and nothing checks `think.ops_limit <= max_ops_backstop`, so a think whose `ops_limit` sat above the backstop would end `limit` at the backstop instead of `ops` at its ceiling (the largest class ceiling as the backstop with `CoreProfile`); the `line_to` cap and the ops ceiling are class I's until `CoreProfile`; the stand-in on a drop is native T1 with `DROPPED_LINE` until the ROM, and a dropped think records no `ThinkStat`; a possess-then-release lays down a fresh row and un-drops a dropped hull, and a snapshot decode reinstalls `default_mind_runner` (the dormant row and the seal recording the runner); `label()` ranks a latched refusal above `Fault::Panic`, the reverse of `mush-mind`'s own order, unreachable while no registered production function panics, for the `MindFault` journal to settle; the script does not normalise a foreign or ill-shaped memory as `StockMemory::from` does (the prologue runs `fresh()` only when `state` is absent), unreachable in M2 but a question for the mind switch; `Fault::Memory(MemError::Key(k))` carries the script-written key at full length, bounded by the engine's 8,192-byte string backstop and not by `TRACE_WIDTH`; `runner.rs`' `context::take()` fallback builds a fresh context and would answer a clean think if the thread-local were ever cleared mid-think (an M4 worker, a future context function) — failing closed with a fault word is safer. **Test and fixture debts** — the rally and the withdraw dwell occur in no pooled, mixed or authored lane (0 thinks in each) and are held only by the in-source mirror `m2_mirror_the_withdraw_dwell_and_the_rally`, and `unarmed_withdrew` is one hull at one seed, so a tuning change can turn that nonzero assert red for reasons unrelated to Gate A; lane 3 reaches only Engage and Hold plus the release and the deadline in its 120 s, so Advance, Withdraw, Screen, Support, a mind's own early ends, `ignore_until_ms` and the supporter's leash are never thought on a twelve-row view (about five script minutes, likely `#[ignore]`d with a named step, or a changed table would reach them); `support::lance_intents` writes `issuer: None` and a `Persona` author and lane 3 does not pin `ArenaTempo::Engaged`, so the orders reach the minds as `tests/minds.rs` issues them, not literally as the harness's lance lane does; lanes 1, 2 and the extra lane build their twins from `ArenaConfig::default()` rather than `arena.arena_cfg` (lane 3, `conditions.rs` and `minds.rs` use the arena's), so a departure would surface as a record mismatch; the `conditions.rs` twin and Rhai runs trigger on a Debug-name sniff of the arena's runner, bounded by `assert_twinned`; `support::script_figures` answers depth 0 when the script will not compile at any depth up to 24, so lane 3's `depth <= 24` cannot fail and the real gate is `tests/content.rs`' pin; the state-literal text pin scans only `this.state =`/`==`/`!=` and skips the dispatch's switch-arm labels; `tests/twin.rs` keeps its own stepping loop (`play_on`) beside the lifted `support::play`; `TwinRunner::counting`, `TwinThought.fire_only`, `helm_half`, `printout` and `failure` have no caller since M2.6 and a later lane could quietly re-adopt the looser classification; `support::under_the_twin`'s thread-local is not cleared on a panicking fixture body (safe under libtest's thread-per-test); the mixed lane's printed `unarmed_disabled=36` counts `unit_disabled`/`unit_destroyed` ledger lines, not hulls (the compared Disabled-edge thinks, 120, are the real count); `Muster::mind_unarmed` clears only `CustodyUnit.weapons` after `custody_unit` builds it, so an "unarmed" hull's hit and armour figures are a stock tank's with its guns delisted; `aspect_mask`'s `rear` arm is exercised by no mirror or lane; the resumed fixture proves the Rhai runner is installed after the decode but counts no think after the resume, and since T2 == T1 the stitched hashes cannot tell the runners apart; `best_hostile`'s admit predicate in `kernels.rs` is a character-for-character copy of T1's stand closure with no compile-time link (a `pub(crate) stand_admits` lifted into `stock_mind.rs` would close it); a wrong-typed REQUIRED kernel argument (`rand(1.5)`, a FLOAT weight) is rhai's own catchable not-found rather than a latched `type` refusal, declared and not pinned; the label precedence of an earlier latched refusal over a later overflow is pinned only by the in-source `label()` test (the one script shape that reaches it has no arena fixture, and M2.3's handoff text lists the opposite order); three PARITY rows read loosely (`Order.target`/`of` answer the stored `ContactId` INT where the keyframe prints `null` once the row is unheld, `View.notices` is unfiltered where the keyframe drops empty-text notices, `Notice.friendly` reads the view's twelve rows) and the live parity test passes them on printed values; the parity test hand-stages the heat cell, the pod, the degraded receiver, the standing orders and twelve notice shapes the (j) field never produces, and obtains the mind hull's keyframe by seating a watcher pilot on a clone for a zero-length pulse because `Arena::frame_for` is `pub(crate)`; the depth fixture's clean control is `deep(5)` against the `deep(12)` fault, so any call-level cap between 7 and 13 passes (the twelve-level cap is pinned in `mush-mind`); the kernel test's doc says the blue is moved "three cells off" where the code moves it onto red's entry cell, and the pickers are crowded on the entry cell only rather than the motor audit's shape; `ThinkContext::of` has no test of its own and the fixture's `decide_ms` equals the test window's `cell_cm` (1,000); the package methods are pinned by package, not name by name (`PACKAGE_NAMES` fifteen); `api=1` is frozen with `goto` as custom syntax and `Receiver.reach_permille` floored under its name, so a rename is an `API_VERSION` bump and a name-pin move; the panic fixture prints the caught `boom` panics to stderr; no other sub-cut moved a sentence or the keyframe); the gateway twins in M3.6's gate only (the sweep ran every gateway suite). | **M3.1**: (1) `Arena.classes` carries no `#[serde(skip)]`: `Arena` is not serde, so the snapshot's exhaustive destructuring lists it and `rated_class` as re-derived and the decode writes `STOCK` and `STOCK_RATED_CLASS`; until M3.2 carries the table a match checked out under a lawful non-stock table resumes on the stock one, which a scout or drone pilot's slot count (`[tacsim.minds.classes.I] slots = 1`) or a fill mind's period (`fill_controller = "mind"`) can see, against 16b AF.1's resume equivalence, declared on the field and pinned for M3.2 to invert. (2) `Arena.rated_class` is a new non-carried `pub(crate)` field behind `set_rated_class_for_tests`, needed to hold the lever's value until M3.3's seal. (3) No `Think.held_keys` field: `Sandbox::run` derives the held set from the decoded handed memory, so a runner cannot pass the wrong one, through the siblings `check_held`/`from_map_held` over a `BTreeSet<String>` with `check` unchanged; `NativeStockMind` passes nothing because it runs no memory check. (4) Host plumbing the brief did not list (`TacsimMindsCfg.classes: serde_json::Value`, the `config_ext` mapping and its test) because `TacsimMindsCfg` is `deny_unknown_fields` and the template's commented keys would otherwise refuse the boot. (5) The scout fixture's core sits in the hull, because `scout()` inherits `tank()`'s one-section hull with no turret; `laser_tank_unit` is also cored (II, turret) because an in-source test flies it as a mind. (6) Two older note sentences (eccm's M.4 sentence, two of the scout's) were shortened to keep under the 1,024-byte note bound, so those two digests carry more than the core; the cores pack's notes were reworded to placement alone before the pack was ever committed (the heavy's "a turret hit cannot reach it" would contradict M3.6's `degrade_core`), moving its digest and `LINE_TANK_DIGEST` once within the sub-cut. (7) Only `fire` bounds its slot by the row; `stations::arm` takes no slot number. (12) Cosmetic numeric-literal underscores moved; m1a5_the_production_hash_does_not_move keeps its name. **Residuals still open after M3.6** (the six judges' residual texts merged and de-duplicated; what a later sub-cut closed is not repeated — M3.2 carried `Arena.classes` so a non-stock table resumes as captured, M3.4 truncated the row at every drop and keyed a ROM hull's arming capacity on `thinks_on_rom` with `tests/conditions.rs`' truncate fixture on `Arena::truncate_slots`, M3.5 kept the row across a possess so a Mind → Pilot → Mind change neither un-drops nor drops twice, M3.6 folded the carried fields (making `Arena.minds`' "folded per row" doc true), filtered the core words where untrue, counted the twin lane's stand-in thinks and removed M3.1's clone cost, and `resume.rs`' module head gained step 2b). **For the lead, rulings owed**: (1) the strikes `ops` sentence can be false — `MindDropped.fault` is the third think's word alone, so a run `unknown`, `type`, `ops` (the gate fixture's own tape) is told as `over its 1,500-op budget three thinks running` although one think ran over budget; word the ops shape as `three faults running, the last ops`, or write `fault: ops` only when all three were ops (doc 17 §24 names it); (2) `Arena.rated_class` still drives the think side and `set_rated_class_for_tests` can push it away from what the seal recorded (`class = min(STOCK_RATED_CLASS, core)`); no behavioural difference today since cores only degrade, but which cut moves `mind_class` onto `UnitState.seal.class` is unassigned (M4 or M5); (3) `PoolRefusal.would` is the admitted hull's own ceiling, so `This side would think {would}` reads one hull's load as the side's total (doc 17 §9's own example 96,000/100,000/92,000 adds up under neither reading) — a wording ruling before M5 makes it player-reachable; (4) the dormant row's held order outlives its squad: while a hull is possessed `holds_order_for` ignores the row, so the squad's release never reaches it and at the hand-back the mind resumes the revoked order until `expires_ms` with a second `order_expired` journalled — clear `order` at `suspend_mind` or re-check at `adopt_mind` (fixture-reachable only before M4); (5) under an operator's lowered pool `SIM UNIT` counts no fill while check-out counts every unseated hull at Class 0's 400, so a lobby match can pass every choice and be called off at the last `BOARD` with the pool sentence — doc 17 §9's "`BOARD` never fails because of a core" holds at the stock pool alone; (6) the weekly harness's ci-cohort window p99 lens (12 ms) is marginal on this host at HEAD and with every cut alike (the committed figure 10.663 since M3.6); re-bless or re-gate on an idle box is the owner's or the lead's, no builder may move it. **Engine and record**: `SortieId::in_match` is `(match_id << 32) bit-or unit` with no guard, so a `match_id` of 2^32 or more aliases silently against "unique by construction" (unreachable with sequential ids; a `debug_assert!` or a documented bound at the seal); `Arena::close_open_sorties` is `pub` while `close_sortie` is `pub(crate)` — a caller dropping the returned Vec would close sorties with no tape line and `closed` is never folded, every caller today in `step.rs` (make it `pub(crate)`); an end close to the ledger's byte bound: the closes are marked on the seals before their lines are appended and `Ledger::append` refuses each line on its own, so the timeout backstop can mark every seal closed with no `sortie_closed` on the tape and an elimination end within a few hundred bytes of `max_bytes` can leave a `sortie_closed` as the last line against W.2 (the window widened by about 80 bytes per unit; declared, not guarded); at a drop the row keeps its first slot on the failed mind's own terms until the ROM's next arming lands and `rom_takes_slot` takes it back — at most one ROM think plus its landing during which the kept gun fires on terms the ROM never set; `say_mind_dropped` tells nobody, staff included, when `MatchBook` no longer holds the match or the cause word is none of the three (against the brief's "staff always"), that branch and a `Character` principal whose object is gone untested and the bridge test's recipient set not closed; a core lowered to Class 0 and THEN destroyed journals a `core` drop (seal `core_class` above Zero, core gone), so "journals no drop, ever" is true of the lowering alone; the fault line's hash is taken after the row writes and the core drop's before `dropped` is written (both deterministic, declared); `InstallError`'s text (an object id and a 64-character digest, about 190 columns) and `StartError::Pool`'s `{team}: This side would think …` flow into the lobby's whole-centre call-off — unreachable before M4/M5, to be given a callsign and a short form as `CardUnlawful` was; `MindSeal.card: Vec<u8>` is written as a JSON integer array (about 2 KB per unit, the same card repeated) inside the live record that `write_live` rewrites at every snapshot (200 KB per rewrite at the 100-unit target; a base-16 or canonical-text form, or one card per match, a later tidy); `seal_for` recomputes `standard_digest()` over the 42 KB stock script per unit at check-out (300 times in the harness setup; a `OnceLock` or the digest on `SealTerms`); `put_mind_source` is gated on `TacsimStore::enabled`, so a world with the store disabled holds no stock source and would refuse every scripted resume once M4 lands; the content loader does not refuse `destroy_core`/`degrade_core` authored on a unit with no core; the lobby call-off for `CardUnlawful` is about 130 columns and a `StartError` reached through the `board` verb returns `Err` so the centre's call-off is not heard on that path (pre-existing for every `StartError`); `CustodyUnit::sealed` is a `pub` fixture helper in production code with no production caller; `Arena::mind_class` and `core_lost` read a dormant row's `dropped` without a controller check (their callers are gated) and a `Pilot → Reflex` transition over a dormant row removes it (fixture-only); the pilot's frame keeps two odds rows, so a Class II or III mind reads at most the frame's two until M5's `FRAME_VERSION`. **Tests**: no test pins "a Class I core degraded to 0 on a Mind hull: dropped jobs, no `mind_dropped` line" nor "dropped by strikes then core revoked: one line in total", so a change of `core_lost`'s presence test to a class test would pass unseen; the three-faults gate fixture's "every other line's hash unmoved" is proven only against an overflow baseline that journals through the same think arm (the discriminating pin is the in-source slot-state test; no step-level fixture catches a regression that opens `Pending` inside `run_mind_thinks`); M3.2's `gate_pulses` delivers orders in advancing pulses, so `m3_a_resume_between_n_and_n_plus_one_reproduces_the_hash_sequence_at_three_cadences` compares within one cadence although its name claims three (M3.4's `beats` delivers in a zero-dt pulse for that reason), and its control's `parted >= landing_substep` is true by construction with the bite resting on `assert_ne!` plus the asserted empty fresh answer; the `Live` reclaim after a decode is covered only by the pre-existing `m1a5_a_reclaimed_seat_lands_nothing` (the brief's case is built as a Helm `Linkdead` control at a raised epoch); `m3_an_unchanged_record_resumes_with_the_seal_it_was_checked_out_under` proves the unchanged-record claim whole for the second unit alone and never reads the resumed world's book record; `SIM UNIT`'s call into `pool_at_the_choice` is pinned only through the helper's in-source test (deleting the one-line call would leave the suite green, the verb unable to refuse before M5); the mushd delivery test's object-number check scans Ada's whole window since the mark (a fill dbref of 1, 5, 50 or 500 after a boot-content renumbering would fail it spuriously) and injects a hand-built seq-0 chunk through `ArenaTasks::egress_sender`; `m3_a_roster_tank_without_a_core_is_refitted_with_one_at_the_reset` asserts `refitted >= 1` where exactly one was planted; the `@tacsim/pack show` core row and the template's commented class block (the quoted `"0"` key in particular) have no test; `lead_character_of`'s `Boarding` guard with leads on the board, `adopt_mind` laying `seal_digest` at a later Reflex-to-Mind transition and `principal_word`'s `character` fallback are unpinned; the `degrade_core` sentence and the debrief's core variants are pinned in tacsim's own suite only through unit tests and the bot's contract case. **Docs, names and config**: `mushd.toml.example` mirrors neither the template's commented `[tacsim.minds.classes.*]` block nor its three pool keys (the shipped-configs test compares uncommented headers only); `definitions/content.rs` says the cores pack is placed "ninth" (the sixth `SOURCES` row, ninth by count) under a head that still opens "`SOURCES` and `sources` are untouched"; `stock_mind.rs`'s intent bound still reads `4 + STOCK_CONDITION_SLOTS` (now `4 + slots`, at most 10); `tests/minds_rhai.rs`' `boom_sandbox` doc says "the standard one's limits" while it is built at 1,500 and the standard sandbox at 4,500; sandbox test 18's first sentence still describes the above-backstop case now refused pre-run; `config.rs::m1b1_the_floor_is_ten_to_a_hundred_in_steps_of_ten` asserts 10..=950 under its old name; `tests/cockpit.rs::m1b4_fire_when_arms_slots_in_the_arenas_numbers_and_the_count_is_one_sentence` and `m1a5_the_production_hash_does_not_move` keep their names while their transcripts and constants moved; in-source `conditions.rs::m1b1_the_table_numbers_slots_stably` shadows `STOCK_CONDITION_SLOTS` with a local const of the same name; the stop loop's "not an abort" failure message reads oddly on the `Abort` row it gained; some numeric literals in `M1B0_SEAT_LINES` lost their underscores and some in `M1B5_TWO_T1_EVENTS` gained them during the re-record (values equal); the staff `mind_sealed` line quotes the mind and ends with `principal=…` beyond the brief's literal format; D-UM-33's "the component's asset is a titled object" clause is not built (every component binds `asset: None`, the core included). **Where the record went**: doc 17 §24's M3 bullets, §22's landed sentence and the in-place edits to §8, §9, §12 and §13; 26 should-fix and 21 notes, every one taken, the dispositions the note's §9), and split into seven serial sub-cuts bound by its conventions and the orchestrator's addendum (`scratchpad/briefs/M5-conventions.md`, `M5-later-addendum.md`). Each sub-cut went builder (two stages for M5.2, M5.4 and M5.6), a second agent's gate pass on the tree as left, adversarial review, a fixer verifying every finding against the code before any edit, a read-only final checker, the orchestrator's close with its rulings written into the addendum, then `git fetch`, the commit and the push. The reviews found 158 findings and no blocking one: M5.1 32 (three lenses; 9 should-fix), M5.2 24 (three; 10), M5.3 20 (two; 7), M5.4 20 (three; 5), M5.5 13 (two; 1), M5.6 32 (three; 8), M5.7a 17 (two, the sweep and the evidence and the gate's tests; 11), every one verified, each fixed, declared or ruled. NOT in M5." Doc 17 §9's example corrected in the record." (OQ-12) "(implied by the adopted note and the answers): plan and build M5 now (the M4 gate's three weekly runs stay the owner's, independent); M5 deploys once at its gate; the residues cut sequenced against T-104." **OQ-6's "D's designation half recorded as accepted under D-UM-15" is recorded here as an acceptance, not a scope line**: STOP D's designation half — that a supporter no longer prefers what its charge is fighting (a pilot's lock, a machine's target) and takes its own score's — is accepted under D-UM-15 as built since 17b cut M4.6b, with no mirror and no behaviour to restore; the help, the `SUPPORT` acknowledgement and the leader's prompt say so since M5.6, and `tests/reflex.rs` records it since M5.7a. **The five readings of the note's §7, none vetoed by the owner**: (1) the seal line is said only to a slot that chose a mind (C-18; M5.2); (2) `EXIT` keeps `, left` on the board (C-11; M5.3); (3) the `Your mind` block's "from when" and its shot count are the world's clock (M5.6; on `MatchMirror::now_ms`, the D-B1 ruling below), a shot at a hand-over's millisecond counted as the mind's; (4) Q-UM-15's substitution is taken at the seal, so a pilot's own non-Doctrine mind is never sealed in a both-human match (C-7; M5.2; fixture-only in M5, a shape M6 inherits); (5) the card's slider drawing (R-25) and the rule glyphs (R-24) move to M6, under OQ-8 (C-22; M5.4, M5.5). One behaviour change is doc 17's own text, not a reading: an unarmed hull is never refused for the odds floor (C-23). **The two choices the orchestrator decided** (2026-10-11, each on the feasibility review's recommendation, recorded as the orchestrator's, not the owner's): the TUI draws the `MIND` block as the weapons pane's tail, not a fourth Right pane, which would have cost the contacts pane two rows at 120 × 34 and 120 × 30 and painted an empty pane on the replay and watch screens; and `Your mind` takes its profile names and whether the slot chose from a check-out seed (`Fold::with_own_mind`, `with_own_tank`'s precedent), not a `profiles` key on `mind_sealed`, which could not have said whether a `SIM MIND Standard` with no `PROFILE` chose. **The orchestrator's rulings after the note** (each binding, in the addendum or in the sub-cut's record-notes close; the reason beside each). M5.1's close (D-R2): a `SIM UNIT` or `SIM MIND` change that lowers a slot's load is never refused, one that keeps or raises it is asked of the side after it — because an unchecked claim or a `SIM AI <n>` can put a side past its half under a lowered pool, which may then only move down, while an equal-load change still re-asks the side and keeps the later-of evidence; unreachable at the stock constants. M5.2's S1, option (b): a governed core at Class 0 flies the ROM, as no core does (`&& hw.class > Zero` at the choice and the seal) — because Class 0 is doc 17's word for the drive ROM alone (D-UM-35), OQ-10 (d) keeps the over-class refusal fixture-only, and a stuck tank degraded to Class 0 should seal Class 0 as at `5f505c8` rather than call every match off `MindOverClass`; `tests/minds.rs`' `crowded_field` returned to its `5f505c8` body. M5.2's D21: the `af1` walk extended to every objective before the commit, one move in the history (`811a1ce7…`, 556) — because a second re-bless after M5.2 was a STOP and the walk closes doc 17 §24's index item (`Engage.target`, `Screen.facing_mdeg`, `Support.of` as a number), so the format and the pin moved once together. M5.2's D12, D22 and D26, carried as declared, all fixture-only: D12 — Q-UM-15's `SIM START` sentence to a lead is decided once at `SIM START`, so a fill first fielded by the boarding deadline's drop is substituted at the seal without it, because telling every lead whose fill is on and non-Doctrine would say it where no substitution happens, against OQ-5; D22 — the seal line on a coreless hull (or one whose core reads Class 0) says `Class 0`, because that is doc 17's word for the ROM and a ROM-worded fourth shape would be a new sentence and a new bot reader case; D26 — after a substitution the bare `SIM MIND` and `SIM AI MIND` name the CHOICE and `MIND` the seal, because the lobby keeps the entry as it was resolved while the seal line and Q-UM-15's sentence say what flies. M5.2's D23 (settled in the addendum): Q-UM-15's sentence and the seal line go to an audience naming the pilot's own object (`sys_to_actor(pilot, …)`), never `ctx.actor`, because the check-out runs inside the last `BOARD`'s command or the boarding deadline's tick, so the actor is not each pilot (the note's F2). M5.3's D9, option (a): M5.6 owns the order lapse, `ORDER`'s help line and the report's release line, each worded "their minds" — because "doctrine" is false of any other sealed mind and one word for every mind leaks nothing; the leader's system prompt, model-facing, keeps "its own doctrine", and "Standard Doctrine" stays the mind's name. M5.3's D3, option (a), accepted as declared: the plural release acknowledgement passes 120 columns only at a non-shipped `[tacsim.arena].squad_size` of 2 or less (six squads: 128 columns, where the old form read 134; 104 at the shipped config) — because it is never wider than before and needs an operator setting the shipped content never sets. M5.3's D1, accepted: the bot's `Line::Doctrine` renamed `Line::OnItsMind` — because the old name was false and no `Line` is persisted. M5.4's D-B1, option (a): `MIND`'s countdown and pre-checks, and M5.6's report span, read `MatchMirror::now_ms` — because the note's and the conventions' `MatchMirror.arena_ms` is the highest ledger event's stamp, nought for a match's first minutes and lagging a quiet match, while `now_ms` is the arena's own clock as the last `Frames` reported it; the design note's items 4 and 5 named the wrong field, corrected in doc 17 §24. M5.4's D-B10, option (a): `Frame.mind` stays built on every `frame_for`, a mind's view included, which discards it (about 450 to 540 ns a call, at most about 2.5 per cent of a wake's CPU p99 in the minds lanes) — because the conventions bind the build to `frame_for`, doc 17 §3's one builder, and the cost is small; carried as a perf note to the arena-step work (16c `T-104`). M5.4's D-B2: the two ROM head sentences kept (a coreless hull; a seal capped at Class 0 on a real core, unreachable at the shipped `fill_class` I) — because the first line names the ROM wherever the ROM thinks. M5.5's D3 amended, option (c), accepted: the tail's third row reads `off for the match: on the ROM` for a live class of 0 as well as a dropped row — because line mode's D-B2 names the ROM there and a live class only falls in a match. M5.5's D4, option (b): the flown profile is plain text and only the profiles the mind is not flying are buttons — because a switch to the flown one is refused, so offering it was a dead end. M5.5's D8, accepted: no TUI style sets the tail apart (the web keeps 0.35rem of air) — a theme decision. M5.6's RF1, option (a): a staff `@arena/release` or a pod's `SIM LEAVE` while `Running` reads `when you climbed out` in the `Your mind` block — because these are staff-only paths (no third hand-over cause, which would be a new `Slot` field and a new sentence); `SIM LEAVE` is in fact refused outside `Selection` and `Boarding` (`match_/lobby.rs:1471-1475`), so in play only staff's `@arena/release` reaches it. The M5.7 split: two commits as M4's record was done, M5.7a the gate's tests, the last sweep and the gate run, M5.7b this record. And: the pre-existing `mush-server` flake (`world_task::tests::what_still_waits_when_the_loop_ends_is_answered_not_dropped`, about 6 to 9 per cent on the base too) is no M5 sub-cut's — re-run its package once if it fails, never "fixed" here (a separate task was suggested). **The splits.** M5.7 landed in two commits, M5.7a and M5.7b (above); M5.2, M5.4 and M5.6 were each built in two stages inside one commit; the plan's two-line fix `bb31b139` is its own commit. **Departures that change behaviour or words, each declared where it landed** (the rest are in each sub-cut's record notes). M5.1: `Holdings` gains a provided `sources()`; `SIM MIND none` is held to the pool like every set; the board row's width stays unbounded (two pilots on `line_tank_thermal`, one with the reader's named clause and one `, a mind`, measure 126 columns; a row per pilot would move the bot's board reader; D-R4, carried by the orchestrator's ruling); the pool line's half is the choice's own pool (12 units: 244,000 of 488,000, not the note's 238,000); new sentences the note did not list (the card's pair refused at the choice, `SIM AI MIND`'s side word for staff, `…; selection is closed.`, the usage lines, the coreless `flies the ROM` form and `Fits target_mk1 (ROM): …`); the last `BOARD`er reads the call-off's short form for every `StartError` (for the pool, with a fixture's figures, `The match could not start: red's compute (3,000 ops a second) is over its half (1,904).`), the long form logged (D-R3); a second character of a slot's account reads and sets its mind by name but sees `, a mind` on the board (D-R5); staff's `SIM STATUS` is a player's board, staff reading names on `@arena/status` (D-R6). M5.2: `StartError::CardRefused` is new (D24), so no card refusal puts a profile name or a 157-column sentence in the centre's call-off; a scripted seal with no source is refused before the first write (D25); a profile name no preset answers and a set of more than four are `SealError::Card` (D2); a stamp-less switched index folds `i64::MIN` (D3); every landed switch journals `mind_card`, a seated hull with no row too (D8); the substitution's fallback goes through a new `SealTerms.fallback` (D13); `Centre.doctrine_only` is skipped when false (D15); the resume gate test is arena-level (M5.2's D16). M5.3: the negative fog assertions widened to `flown by` and `flying on` so they stay discriminating whatever the word (D4); D7 below (the deploy). M5.4: a wreck's `MIND PROFILE` reads `MIND refused. Your tank is destroyed and takes no orders. EXIT leaves the pod.` (D-B3); a word no preset answers is refused without an echo, `MIND refused. No profile of that name is sealed on this mind; it carries Sniper and Line.` (D-B4); a dropped row keeps its block and its switch (D-B5); sentences the note did not give — the set of one `Profile: Line, 1 of 1; no other is sealed to switch to.` (what every unchosen slot's pilot reads), the in-window form, the `MIND PROFILE <name>` form past two profiles, the usage, and the two-profile switch line bounded at 100 columns, not the note's 99 (D-B7); the bot reads the pre-checks as `Line::Refused { order: "MIND" }`, not neutral lines (D-B8); a switch refused by the world's routing keeps every verb's `Refused: … .` (D-B9). M5.5: a bar (`\|`) for `·` on a terminal that is not UTF-8 (D1); the profile row's spacing read off the note's one example (D2); `core` and `next_switch_ms` decoded, never drawn (D5); `heavy_mk1` with all three guns armed shows no tail at 120 × 30, and a three- or four-profile web row can overflow the narrowest pane (unreachable at Class I). M5.6: the span on the mirror's clock, a shot at a hand-over's millisecond the mind's (D1); two or more switches read `switched N times` with the last on its own line (D2); a substituted seal adds `  Sealed in place of your choice, for humans on the other side.` (D3); spans of differing causes carry no cause clause (D4); the shots line's shape (D5); the drop's cause words `three faults running`, `its core was destroyed` and `its core could not run its mind on this engine` (D6, D17's cause word alone); a span ends at the hull's destruction, one opened after it is none (RF3); the hint said on every end, a void, an abort and a range included (B1); the order tool's `support` description made true beside the prompt (B2); doc 17c's "What moves" still cites `m3_the_staff_read_the_four_mind_lines_and_the_pilot_reads_nothing_of_them` and `ah1_a_first_account_reads_five_hints_…`, renamed `…_reads_its_own_drop_alone` and `…_six_hints_…` (D9, B3). M5.7a: two arena seconds in the seat before the `EXIT` so the cold claim is never vacuous (D1); the tape's order checked to the exit's second, not its moment, the cold `EXIT` witnessed by `STATUS`'s `power off` and the absent `Power online.` (D2, D10); the fog sweep's hex words wider than the note's letter — any all-hex run longer than sixteen that is not all figures, and any all-hex word of six or more mixing figures and letters (D5, D11); the post-start board pinned whole per reader (D12); the re-pointed comments written "… after 17b's M5", so the F16 grep reads only this cut's own three lines and the twelve "17b's M5" sites are read outside it (D7; the Evidence's F16 paragraph). **Deploy** (one deploy at this gate, one void; the note's §5 as corrected by M5.3's D7): the format move voids every match running across the reload `aborted(restart)` ("snapshot format 9 is not 10"), its tanks restored from check-out, nothing migrating; `LIVE_FORMAT` stays 1 (`Slot.mind`, `Centre.fill_minds`, `Centre.doctrine_only`, the report fold's `OwnMind` and spans, `MindSeal.profiles` all `#[serde(default)]`); `MIND_ABI` and `STANDARD_SHA256` stand, so no seal is refused by name; `FRAME_VERSION` stands at 3 and an older web client ignores the `mind` key, but the web build ships with the server. **Deploy the bot from the same commit**: an older bot still follows a running match, because its pod string carries the new clause (`Red Pod 1, flown by its mind`, its standing `None`), but it reads the reconnect line `… is flying on its mind; BOARD takes it back.` as `Line::Other` and learns it is out of the tank only from `OUTSIDE_COCKPIT` — not the note's "loses its resume" (D7, verified by M5.3's review against `mush-bot`'s `Centre::Running` arm); the lapse's new tail is a neutral line to either bot. The drone pack's version 2 → 3 is a content reload; no production hash pin moved, so the harness baseline and the owner's weekly runs stand. What the deploy changes for a pilot: the choice in the lobby (`SIM MIND`, `SIM AI MIND`, the pool's new sentence), the seal line, the board's, the reconnect's and the release's words, the `MIND` verb and block on line mode, the TUI and the web, the report's `Your mind` block, the sixth hint, `help minds`, an unarmed hull no longer refused for a raised odds floor (C-23), and the help saying what the machines do (they do not contest a Hold match's spot; a supporter fights what it finds) until the residues cut restores the behaviour. **Residuals still open**: the note's D16 (`MindSeal.card` a JSON integer array, owed; doc 17c §2.2); the note's D17 (`MindDropped.fault`'s ruling open; the block prints the cause word alone); **the residues cut** after M5, named here 17b cut M5r (the note's §6.1 left the name to the committer) (STOP A by a public keyframe key with `FRAME_VERSION` 3 → 4, STOPs F, G, I and K, M4.5a's consequences if the owner wants them, `standard.rhai`'s stale header and its "fourteen keys", the help, the acknowledgement, the drone note and the prompt restored with the behaviour, one `STANDARD_SHA256`, one `MIND_ABI` and one declared production re-bless, sequenced against 16c `T-104` and never interleaved; the seal, the floor, over-class, STOP E's charge and the format move; the board's words and the help; the `MIND` verb, its block and the report); 16c `T-104`'s sequencing note (the residues cut, by its name, and D-B10's cost; no 16c row closes) and an italic pointer on the Y sketch's `flown by doctrine` (M5.3's and M5.7a's carried); 17b §2 gains no row, the harness block never having moved. |

## 4. M5rules continuation gate — 2026-10-17

**Status:** accepted on 2026-10-17 at `e93a4a1c`. Implementation, independent review,
Windows verification and Linux replay/worker gates are complete. The Linux run
used the same source in an isolated directory on conclav.io, whose use for testing
and dependencies the owner explicitly authorized. No deployment is part of this
cut; this acceptance does not accept M5r or M6.

**Scope:** 17d §2's three coupled parts: bounded typed ordered native rules,
station/epoch admission, canonical pilot grammar and English, cockpit/keyframe and
Rhai projection, TUI/web/bot readers, help, and compatibility. Fire and hold use
Fire authority; lock uses Sensors authority. Unknown target facts stay false.
Conditional holds and ORDER hold-fire veto automatic fire; the first matching
authored action wins without falling through a cycling selected gun. Doctrine's
independent guns use the same bounded table. Rejected ready authored Fire rules
retain the existing own-hull held-shot explanations and ten-second limiter without
firing, leaking target facts, or warning for an earlier row when a later authored
row wins. Real SSH observer tests cover this regression.

**Compatibility:** cockpit frame 4; mind API 2; stock version 1;
`rhai=1.26.0;api=2;stock=1`; snapshot format 11. The ordered table is hashed and
strictly decoded. No old-snapshot/old-ABI migration was added. Standard's source
SHA-256 is `55cabde517b6a08e3d6e8017a43d7231c639c1d8ebfbe6e3777b52ed18115227`.
The API inventories are 254 registrations, 220 resolution calls and 140 parity
getters. A complete primitive projection fixture tests the new rule fields;
bounded getter batches avoid inflating production memory limits to fit the probe.

**Review:** the core/rule evaluator, public projection/readers, parser/cockpit
surfaces and compatibility fixtures were cross-reviewed by agents that did not
write those portions. Reviews found and corrected precedence, authority/fog,
readiness diagnostic and fixture issues before this gate. The final lint-only
change replaces a test's forbidden `unreachable!` with an exhaustive enum; its
regression was rerun afterward.

**Windows verification:**

- Full workspace sweep: 396 suite reports, 7,462 passing tests, 21 failures and
  92 ignored tests. The failures were investigated individually: version/identity
  and canonical-reader expectations, a bounded API-probe fixture, the actual
  held-shot diagnostic regression, and a gateway supervision test that passed
  alone. Two doctest processes also lost dependency artifacts during overlapping
  Cargo builds; the isolated doctest run below is authoritative.
- Final affected-target recheck: **3,324 passed, zero failed, three ignored** in
  56 suite reports, including every workspace library and the observer, solo,
  weapons, arena, cockpit, deliberation, mind, Rhai, motor, reflex and conditions
  integration targets. Gateway supervision's isolated rerun passed. Final
  doctests: **18 passed, zero failed, three ignored**, across 45 crate reports.
- Separate external bot suite: **706 passed, zero failed, five ignored**. TUI:
  **25 passed**. Web: **47 suites / 583 tests passed**, Svelte check zero errors
  and warnings, production build passed. Canonical cockpit JSON/text fixtures
  were generated by the real Rust fixture and checked again; four TUI goldens
  moved with the canonical rules line.
- All-target workspace Clippy with `-D warnings` and strict workspace rustdoc
  passed. The latter required 53 inherited comment-only repairs across 13 files;
  they change no economy/runtime code. `cargo fmt --all --check` exceeds the
  Windows command-line limit, so `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` checked every
  changed Rust file, including the new rules module. `git diff --check` passed.
- Final named CI gates: cross-platform corpus passes on Windows (the four
  stopping times are unchanged; 3,600 float-probe thinks retain bitmask
  369098751); native/Rhai whole-match equality covers **15 matches, 30 plays,
  11,096 substeps and 9,563 ledger lines with zero divergences**; the real worker
  pool covers **45 matches**. Snapshot shape/resume tests passed.

**Linux verification:** `target/m5rules-linux-final.log` records the complete SSH
run on conclav.io with rustc 1.98.0. The uploaded `m5rules-source.tar.gz` SHA-256 is
`ea5bd5e533be68f6d31b79ce78bbdbfe950f36ef7eba17ca09d065b109d037d6`.
All four cross-host tuples (stopping time, final state hash, ledger digest and
message digest) match Windows; the 3,600-think float probe retains bitmask
369098751. Native/Rhai equality passes **15 matches, 30 plays, 11,096 substeps and
9,563 ledger lines with zero divergences**. The real task/worker-pool gate passes
**45 matches**. These are correctness gates; their host timings are report-only.

Commands used the pinned toolchain, `--offline` and `-j 2` where applicable:

```text
cargo test --workspace --exclude mush-bot --no-fail-fast
cargo test --workspace --exclude mush-bot --lib --test observer_rule --test sim_solo --test sim_weapons --test arena --test cockpit --test deliberation --test minds --test minds_rhai --test motor --test reflex --test weapons --test conditions --no-fail-fast
cargo test --workspace --exclude mush-bot --doc --no-fail-fast
cargo test -p mush-bot
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc --locked --workspace --no-deps --document-private-items
cargo test -p tacsim --test minds_rhai m2_the_twin_corpus_hashes_the_same_on_every_host -- --ignored --exact --nocapture
cargo test -p tacsim --test twin m2_whole_match_hashes_are_equal_between_the_runners_over_the_pool -- --ignored --exact --nocapture
cargo test -p tacsim --test minds_pool m4_real_tasks_over_the_twin_s_thirty_hash_as_the_library -- --ignored --exact --nocapture
```

The final three ignored tests used the freshly compiled test binaries directly,
with tacsim as the working directory, to avoid another Cargo feature-set rebuild.
The release harness likewise used one freshly built executable for the CI arena,
authored-reach and all four minds variants, with `TACSIM_ASSERT` unset. Every
variant was sound, with zero faults/overflows. Timing is report-only under the
owner's ruling; these Windows measurements also overlapped debug replay work.

**Deterministic baseline:** all behavioral counters, operation totals, thresholds
and state histograms match the existing VPS baseline. Only the reviewed new
source/typed-state identity changes four `minds.*.final_substep_hash` leaves and
the decimal serialized ledger length, **1,637,062 → 1,636,915 bytes**. These five
leaves were updated with explicit provenance in `blessed.deterministic_update`;
the original VPS timing measurements and host identity remain intact. The actual
harness delta compared 106 arena fields and the four minds blocks (102, 30, 30
and 34 fields) equal after that update. Because the harness selects its baseline
by host OS, this one check used a temporary Windows filename for the Linux file,
removed afterward; the different-host timing branch correctly skipped comparison.

**Carries:** M5r owns the
doctrine/motor restoration and its next compatibility change; M6 owns authoring,
